Title: A Cry from the Desart: or, Testimonials of the Miraculous Things lately come to pass in the Cevennes, verified upon oath, and by other proofs; translated from the originals, with a preface by John Lacy; the second edition
Creator: François Maximilien Misson (compiler); translated, with a preface, by John Lacy
Publisher: London: printed by B. Bragg (imprint partly legible in the scan)
Publication Date: 1707
Rights: Public Domain (published 1707, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_eighteenth-century_a-cry-from-the-desart-o_misson-maximilien_1707_0 (https://archive.org/details/bim_eighteenth-century_a-cry-from-the-desart-o_misson-maximilien_1707_0), the item's own bim_eighteenth-century_a-cry-from-the-desart-o_misson-maximilien_1707_0_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Internet Archive; host metadata date: 1707. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: 53db2f20e6e49cc22e1fc5b4efba6b94deb96e494190b34c25581363eab86e4a.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. British Library microfilm scan (Eighteenth Century Collections Online), second edition; the title page and the first page are garbled, the testimonies readable with the long s kept.
Content note: The English version of the Théâtre sacré, made by the English prophet John Lacy in the year of the French original: the witnesses' testimonies on oath before the Masters in Chancery, with Lacy's preface and an advertisement listing the deponents.

Since many People have enquired of me, the reaſon 


and occaſion that brought me into England, I here rea- 


dily give an account of it; after I was deliver'd out 


of Priſon at Perpignian, by the Capitulation made with . 


Mr. Cavalier (having there been long derained be- 


cauſe of my Inſpirations) my Father ſent me to a Kinf. 


man at Montpellier, to be aſſiſtant to him in his buſfi- ” 


neſs ; a Report being raiſed, that I was concern'd in 
the Affair fallen out at Nrſmes,the diſmal ſtory of which 
is well known, my Father thought good to ſend me 


out of the way, to draw me out of the dangers at-

not diſcover d; the World may make what reflexions

In the Month of Oftober or November, 1703. (as I 
believe) our Troop being at the Caſtle of Gardzes near 
Pierredon, one named Languedoc, a Serjeant in the Re-

be would for time to come, fight among us for the 
Cauſe of God; ſome of our Troop knew he was of

their ſeveral Members are yeilded up; and here to 
prevent Miſtakes, I do again repeat, that in both thoſe 
Wonderful Inſtances I am in no wiſe the Agent, I do

paooſe of my Body and Soul which are thi

unto him, tis He and His great adorable Power, thar
